Supplement 0.E
Embedding context-free session types

This section presents an informal investigation of two questions that relate AlgST and
context-free session types (CFST

Almeida et al. 2022
): can we map any AlgST program into an equivalent CFST program? And: can we map any CFST program to an
equivalent AlgST program?

This investigation is necessarily informal because the features of
AlgST and CFST do not line up. AlgST is a minimalist mostly linear
version of System F with products, sessions, and algebraic
protocols. CFST is also based on System F, but has a richer kind
structure that enables unrestricted as well as linear features,
variant and record types, and the full slate of context-free session
primitives.

To enable a meaningful comparison, we focus on the linear fragment of
CFST
Almeida et al. 2022

using the notation of
Costa et al. 2022
,
restricted to pairs and ignoring De Bruijn indices.
In AlgST, we only consider types that are not polymorphic in
protocols because CFST has no equivalent. We add linear algebraic
datatypes to cope with variant types in CFST.

Non-parameterized algebraic sessions into (linear) context-free sessions

The translation from
algebraic session types that do not parameterize over protocol types
(AlgST
0
) to context-free session types
(CFST) is given by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T}}}\Rbag}}
, which is defined on
types in normal form, only:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{Unit}}}}\Rbag}}=\texttt{unit}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T\to U}}}\Rbag}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T}}}\Rbag}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{U}}}\Rbag}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T\otimes U}}}\Rbag}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T}}}\Rbag}}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{U}}}\Rbag}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\alpha}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\colon}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}{\textbf{{t}}}}}}}}{.T}}}}\Rbag}}=\forall\alpha:{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}{\textbf{{t}}}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}{\textbf{{lin}}}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T}}}\Rbag}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\alpha}}}\Rbag}}=\alpha\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{?T}.{U}}}}\Rbag}}=?{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T}}}\Rbag}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{U}}}\Rbag}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{!T}.{U}}}}\Rbag}}=!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T}}}\Rbag}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{U}}}\Rbag}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{End}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{!}}}}\Rbag}}=\texttt{skip}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{End}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{?}}}}\Rbag}}=\texttt{skip}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{?{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{P}\penalty\ {\overline{U}}}}}.{S}}}}\Rbag}}=P_{U};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{S}}}\Rbag}}\text{ where }P_{U}\doteq\&\{C_{i}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathsection(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{-}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\overline{T_{i}[\overline{U}/\overline{\alpha}]}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{)}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{).}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{End}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{!}}}}}}\Rbag}}\}_{i\in I}\text{ for }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{protocol}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\rho}}\ \overline{\alpha}}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{C}}_{i}}}\ \overline{T_{i}}}\}_{i\in I}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{P}\penalty\ {\overline{U}}}}}.{S}}}}\Rbag}}=P_{U};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{S}}}\Rbag}}\text{ where }P_{U}\doteq\oplus\{C_{i}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathsection(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{+}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{(}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\overline{T_{i}[\overline{U}/\overline{\alpha}]}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{)}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{).}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{End}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{!}}}}}}\Rbag}}\}_{i\in I}\text{ for }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{protocol}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\rho}}\ \overline{\alpha}}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{C}}_{i}}}\ \overline{T_{i}}}\}_{i\in I}}}

Functional types and
message exchanges are naturally mapped onto their counterparts. The end of a session is mapped
to
skip
, whereas algebraic protocols are translated to
type names defined as internal choices or branches, depending on whether they appear
in sending or receiving positions. The choice labels coincide with the protocol
constructors.

We claim that if
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T}}\equiv_{A}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{U}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T}}}\Rbag}}\simeq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Lbag{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{U}}}\Rbag}}
.

CFST to AlgST: Translation of the functional fragment

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T\colon\mathsf{T^{lin}}}\rrbracket}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{T}}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}{\textbf{{t}}}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{\texttt{unit}}\rrbracket}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{Unit}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T\rightarrow U}\rrbracket}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{U}\rrbracket}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T\times U}\rrbracket}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}\otimes{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{U}\rrbracket}}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{\langle\ell\colon T_{\ell}\rangle_{\ell\in L}}\rrbracket}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X_{L}}}\text{ where }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{data}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X_{L}}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{\ell}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T_{\ell}}\rrbracket}}}\}_{\ell\in L}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{X\colon\kappa}\rrbracket}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X}}\text{ for }X\doteq T\text{ and }\kappa\neq\mathsf{S^{lin}}\text{ where }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{data}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{\mathsf{Unfold}_{X}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T\colon\mathsf{S^{lin}}}\rrbracket}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{!X_{T}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{End}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{!}}}}}}\text{ where }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{protocol}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X_{T}}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{X_{T}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{T}\rrparenthesis}}}}\text{ and $X_{T}$ is a fresh name.}

CFST to AlgST:Translation of the session typed fragment

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{T\colon\mathsf{S^{lin}}}\rrparenthesis}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\overline{T\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}{\textbf{{p}}}}}}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{\texttt{skip}}\rrparenthesis}}=\varepsilon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{!T}\rrparenthesis}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{?T}\rrparenthesis}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{-{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{T;U}\rrparenthesis}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{T}\rrparenthesis}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{U}\rrparenthesis}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{\oplus\{\ell\colon T_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}}\rrparenthesis}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X_{L}}}\text{ where }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{protocol}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X_{L}}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{\ell}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{T_{\ell}}\rrparenthesis}}}\}_{\ell\in L}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{\&\{\ell\colon T_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}}\rrparenthesis}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{-{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X_{L}}}}}}\text{ where }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{protocol}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X_{L}}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{\ell}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{\texttt{dual}\,T_{\ell}}\rrparenthesis}}}\}_{\ell\in L}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{X\colon\mathsf{S^{lin}}}\rrparenthesis}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X}}\text{ for }X\doteq T\text{ where }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{protocol}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{\mathsf{Unfold}_{X}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{T}\rrparenthesis}}}}

Figure 13
:
Translation of linear context-free session types to algebraic session types

Linear context-free sessions embedded into algebraic sessions

In this direction, we omit polymorphism to obtain a lighter, more
perspicuous notation. Our comparative approach scales to the
polymorphic setting, but at the price of high notational overhead.

The translation of the
linear
and
monomorphic
fragment of CFST
to AlgST is split between the functional and the session typed fragments,
which are mutually defined and presented in
fig.

13
. The translation of a
context-free session type
T
to the algebraic setting is given by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}
.
The functional operators have natural counterparts in the algebraic side.
When translated to the isorecursive setting, functional recursive types are represented
by a datatype enclosed with an explicit unfold. Variant types are mapped to datatypes whose
constructors are the respective labels. Session types are materialized by protocol definitions:
each session type originates a protocol emerging from the conversion of a sequential composition of
types into a sequence of (algebraic) types with kind
p
.
This conversion is governed by function
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{\cdot}\rrparenthesis}}
that maps
skip

to the empty sequence and uses polarities to enforce the direction of communication.
In the session typed fragment, recursive types and choices are represented by protocol definitions.
Type names are considered generative and we relax AlgST to admit
overloaded constructor names that can be reused across protocol definitions.

It remains to define suitable adapters in the places where CFST relies
on type equivalence of equirecursive types. To this end we generate
isomorphisms as witnesses of equivalence proofs using the rule system
proposed by
Costa et al. 2022
.
The tricky part of constructing such an isomorphism is its extension
into session types.
For simplicity, we consider just one side of the isomorphism

\mathsf{iso}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{U}\rrbracket}}

where
T\simeq U
are session types:

⬇

iso

t

=

let

(
u
,

u
’)

=

new

\llbracket
U
\rrbracket

in

fork

$

\delta

t

u
’

\rhd

u

This definition relies on a process
\delta:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{Dual}}}\penalty\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{U}\rrbracket}}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{Unit}}}
that consumes two (dual) channel
ends and implements a forwarder that compensates for the differences
between
T
and
U
. The witness processes
\delta
are
defined for each pair of equivalent session types, according
to the rules. We sketch some witness
processes in
table

1
and provide a more detailed analysis of the isomorphism.

For the sake of simplicity, in all cases in
table

1
, we elide an initial administrative
match

and
select
step resulting from the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T\colon\mathsf{S^{lin}}}\rrbracket}}
.
The case for
T\simeq U
where
T=\texttt{Skip}
and
U=\texttt{Skip}
would be
formally defined by:

⬇

\delta

t

u

=

match

u

with

{

XU

u

\rightarrow

let

t

=

select

XT

t

in

wait

u

\rhd

terminate

t

}

Process
\delta

match
es with
X_{U}
on channel
u
and
select
s
X_{T}
on channel
t
, as prescribed by the
protocol definitions resulting from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{\texttt{Skip}}\rrbracket}}
(see
fig.

13
). According
to the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{\texttt{Skip}}\rrbracket}}
and
E-Match
, we are left with type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{End}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{?}}}

on
u
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{End}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{!}}}
on
t
, so we
wait
on
u
and
terminate
on
t
.

In general,
\delta
inserts the explicit unfolds imposed by the translation to the isorecursive setting.
We showcase our approach for the equivalence
Y\simeq U
where type
Y
is defined by
T
,
Y\doteq T
.
The definition of
\delta
follows the equivalence rule of
Costa et al. 2022

as shown below.
A witness process
\delta
for the embedding
\mathsf{iso}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{Y}\rrbracket}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{U}\rrbracket}}

builds on a process
\delta_{1}
witnessing
T\simeq U
and is defined by:

⬇

\delta

y

=

match

u

with

{

XU

u

\rightarrow

let

=

select

XY

in

let

=

UnfoldY

in

\delta

}

\frac{Y\doteq T\quad T\,\mathsf{contr}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{{\delta_{1}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\colon}}T\simeq U}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{{\delta}}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\colon}}Y\simeq U}

As a result of the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{Y\colon\mathsf{S^{lin}}}\rrbracket}}
,
process
\delta

match
es with
X_{U}
on channel
u
and

s

on channel

, as prescribed by the

(see

). Then,

introduced by

and

to consume the leftovers at

and

.
In the case
X\simeq U
where
X\doteq T
, we are provided with a witness
\delta_{1}
for
T\simeq U
.
We
select
the explicit
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{\mathsf{Unfold}_{X}}}
introduced by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{X}\rrparenthesis}}
and then
use
\delta_{1}
to consume the leftovers at
t
and
u
.

For the case of
!T;V\simeq{}!U;W
, we are provided not only with a witness
\delta_{1}
to consume the remainder
of channels
v
and
w
, but also with a function
\theta\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{U}\rrbracket}}

that witnesses the isomorphism resulting from
T\simeq U
. The functions witnessing the isomorphism
between types in the functional fragment are presented in
table

2
.
Given
\delta_{1}
and
\theta
, after the initial administrative
match
and
select
,
process
\delta

receive
s
u
on channel
w
,
transforms

with
\theta^{-1}
and sends the result through channel
v
. Process

\delta_{1}
consumes the remainder of
v
and
w
.

To witness the isomorphism corresponding to
!T;V\simeq{}!U
where
V
is a
terminated session (see
Costa et al. 2022
for further details), we are given
a process
\sigma
that consumes the explicit unfolds resulting from the isorecursive interpretation of
V
. The

consumer
processes
\sigma
for terminated sessions are presented in

.

The other cases in
tables

2
,
1
and
3
follow
a similar reasoning. The omitted cases for monomorphic types also fit the same approach. For the polymorphic
fragment, we would need to consider a
reader monad
where one could store a map from type
variables to the processes that consume the corresponding channels or transform the
corresponding values, depending on whether the type variables are of kind
s
or not.
In the axiom for polymorphic (non-session typed) variables
n\simeq n
, for instance,
we could think of
\theta
as the identity function, but we wouldn’t know which type to
assign to the bound variable:
\theta={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{\lambda{x}\colon{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{?}}}.{x}}}
. Whereas to consume two channels
typed with a polymorphic session-typed variable, we would need to know how to consume both
endpoints.
Processes to consume or transform values typed with polymorphic variables would need to
be provided to
\delta
. For the translation of types in the polymorphic fragment, we would need to collect the free
variables occurrig in the type and use a parametrized
data
or
protocol
definition:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{X\colon\kappa}\rrbracket}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X\ \overline{\alpha}}}\text{ for }X\doteq T\text{ and }\kappa\neq\mathsf{S^{lin}}\text{ where }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{data}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X\ \overline{\alpha}}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{\mathsf{Unfold}_{X}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\operatorname{fv}}}({T})=\overline{\alpha}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{X\colon\mathsf{S^{lin}}}\rrparenthesis}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X\ \overline{\alpha}}}\text{ for }X\doteq T\text{ where }{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\texttt{protocol}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{X\ \overline{\alpha}}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{=}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}{\mathsf{Unfold}_{X}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llparenthesis{T}\rrparenthesis}}}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\operatorname{fv}}}({T})=\overline{\alpha}

Although we believe that polymorphic types
do not pose any problem to this analysis, we decided to keep the notation simple and
focus on the monomorphic fragment.

In this analysis we only provided witnesses for the isomorphism in one direction.
For the
other direction of the isomorphism, we could follow a similar approach for defining

\mathsf{iso}^{-1}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{U}\rrbracket}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\llbracket{T}\rrbracket}}

and then prove that
\mathsf{iso}
and
\mathsf{iso}^{-1}
compose giving the identity.
